349557 Let $H$ be a graph. A graph is **norming** if its associated graph norm is a norm, and a **stable reflection graph** is a graph arising as the specified $(G_1,G_2;G)$-graph, where $G$ is a finite reflection graph and $G_1,G_2$ are parabolic subgroups with $|G_1\cap G_2|=1$.

**Conlon's conjecture.** Every norming graph is a stable reflection graph. Equivalently, a graph is norming if and only if it is isomorphic to a special type of $(G_1,G_2;G)$-graph with these properties.

The conjecture proposes a complete correspondence between the functional-analytic property of being norming and a group-theoretic description; it would also imply the stated characterization using edge-transitivity under stable involutions. The supplied text does not state whether the conjecture has been resolved.
349572 Let $2\le N\le q-2$. In $\mathrm{PG}(N,q)$, a **normal rational curve** is a $(q+1)$-arc projectively equivalent to

$$
\{(1,t,t^2,\ldots,t^N):t\in\mathbb{F}_q\}\cup\{(0,\ldots,0,1)\}.
$$

A $(q+1)$-arc is **complete** if no further point can be added while preserving the arc property. **Normal rational curve completeness conjecture.** Every normal rational curve in $\mathrm{PG}(N,q)$ is a complete $(q+1)$-arc except when $q$ is even and $N\in\{2,q-2\}$, in which case one point can be added to the curve. This is a geometric formulation of the corresponding extremal-length question for MDS codes; the stated exceptions are precisely the cases in which an additional point may exist.
349582 Let $KG_n$ be the Knödel graph on even $n$ vertices, and let $gamma(KG_n)$ denote its domination number. Let $p$ be an odd prime with $p\leq\lceil\log n\rceil$, and suppose that $2$ is a primitive root modulo $p$. **The first domination upper-bound conjecture.** It then follows that

$$
\gamma(KG_n)\leq\left\lceil\frac{n}{p}\right\rceil.
$$

This conjecture would relax the corresponding theorem's requirement that $p$ divide $n$; the expected dominating sets would use translations by the remainder of $n$ upon division by $p$.
349646 An **IO-graph** is a planar graph obtained by adding an independent set of vertices to the interior faces of an outerplanar graph. Every subgraph of a wheel is an IO-graph.

**IO-graph coupled-choosability conjecture.** Every IO-graph is 5-coupled-choosable.

This class lies between wheel subgraphs and planar partial 3-trees in the paper's investigation. The source does not provide a resolution of this conjecture, so its status remains open.
349655 Let $K_{l,n}$ be the complete bipartite graph with parts of sizes $l$ and $n$, and let $w_{\mathfrak{sl}_2}$ denote the $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ weight system.

**Lando's complete-bipartite reformulation.** The value of the $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ weight system at $K_{l,n}$ is a polynomial of degree $\min(l,n)$.

The paper presents this as a restatement of Lando's chord-diagram conjecture and verifies the cases with $l=1,2,3$ through explicit generating-function calculations. The general assertion remains open in the supplied text.
349689 Let $\mathcal{M}$ be a strongly connected linear compartmental model that is generically locally identifiable, with singular-locus equation $f$. An edge $j\to i$ is **dividing** if its parameter $k_{ij}$ divides $f$. **Dividing-edge conjecture.** If $j\to i$ is a dividing edge of $\mathcal M$, and the model $\mathcal{M}'$ obtained by deleting the edge $j\to i$ is strongly connected, then $\mathcal{M}'$ is unidentifiable. The conjecture is motivated by the converse of the known result that deleting non-dividing edges preserves generic local identifiability. It is proved in cases where deleting the edge increases the shortest input-output path length, but the general claim remains open.
349715 Let $m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}$ with $3\leq m<n$ and $(m,n)\neq(3,4)$. Consider the Diophantine equation

$$
P_m(x)=P_n(y).
$$

**Finiteness conjecture for $P_m$ and $P_n$.** This equation has only finitely many solutions in non-negative integers.

The conjecture is motivated by the paper's results on equal values of partition functions, with $(m,n)=(3,4)$ excluded because it is an exceptional case. The general finiteness assertion remains open.
349863 Let $a,b,\ell,r$ be positive integers with $a\leq b$ and

$$
\frac{r}{r-1}\left(1-\frac{a-1}{a\ell}\right)\geq 1.
$$

Let $K_{a,b}^{\ell}$ denote the graph considered in the paper. **Generalized niceness conjecture.** Under these conditions, $K_{a,b}^{\ell}$ is $r$-nice. The conjecture proposes a further generalization of Theorem~; the paper notes that additional case analysis and new ideas are needed, and does not establish the claim in full.
350093 Let $\mathcal{C}$ be a permutation class, and let $\mathcal{C}^{+1}$ denote its one-point extension. **The 2-wqo one-point-extension conjecture.** If $\mathcal{C}$ is 2-wqo, then $\mathcal{C}^{+1}$ is wqo. The assertion would follow from Pouzet's 2-wqo conjecture, since the proof in the source shows that every 4-wqo class has a wqo one-point extension; it also follows from the stronger conjecture that every 2-wqo class is lwqo.
350096 Let $\mathbf{t}$ be an aperiodic episturmian word. Its **Diophantine exponent** is denoted by $\operatorname{dio}(\mathbf{t})$, and its partial quotients are those associated with its directive sequence.

**Diophantine-exponent conjecture.**

$$
\operatorname{dio}(\mathbf{t})<\infty \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \mathbf{t}\text{ has bounded partial quotients}.
$$

This would extend the corresponding result for regular episturmian words to all aperiodic episturmian words. The claim is presented as a conjecture and remains open in the source.
350144 Let $G$ and $H$ be graphs without isolated vertices. For a graph $X$, let $d_t(X)$ denote its total domatic number, and let $G\mathbin\square H$ denote the Cartesian product of $G$ and $H$.

**Total domatic product conjecture.** One has

$$
d_t(G\mathbin\square H)\leq 2\max\{d_t(G),d_t(H)\}+1.
$$

The paper notes that examples such as $G=K_{2n+1}$ attain $d_t(G\mathbin\square G)=2d_t(G)+1$. The conjectured upper bound is presented as open; a weaker version using ordinary domatic numbers is also posed as an open problem.
350145 Let $G$ and $H$ be graphs without isolated vertices. For a graph $X$, let $d(X)$ denote its domatic number, and let $G\mathbin\square H$ denote the Cartesian product of $G$ and $H$.

**Domatic product conjecture.** One has

$$
d(G\mathbin\square H)\leq 2\max\{d(G),d(H)\}+1.
$$

The source describes this as an open version of the total-domatic conjecture and states that, as far as the authors know, the corresponding conjecture for domatic number is also open.
350181 Let $cmathfrak{S}_n$ be the symmetric group on $n$ elements. A **bistatistic** is an ordered pair of permutation statistics, and two bistatistics are **equidistributed** if they have the same value distribution on $cmathfrak{S}_n$. The statistics $\operatorname{des}_2$ and $\operatorname{pex}$ denote descents of type $2$ and pure excedances, while $\operatorname{des}$ and $\operatorname{exc}$ denote descents and excedances.

**Vajnovszki's conjecture.** The two bistatistics $(\operatorname{des}_2,\operatorname{des})$ and $(\operatorname{pex},\operatorname{exc})$ are equidistributed on $cmathfrak{S}_n$.

The paper presents this as the second conjecture attributed to Vajnovszki. Its status is not resolved in the supplied text.
350182 For each $n$, let $cmathfrak{S}_n$ be the symmetric group on $n$ elements, and let $\operatorname{des}_2$ and $\operatorname{ear}$ be the descent-of-type-$2$ and ear statistics on permutations. The distribution of a pair of statistics is **symmetric** when its associated bivariate generating polynomial is invariant under exchanging the two variables.

**Symmetry conjecture.** The distribution of $(\operatorname{des}_2,\operatorname{ear})$ over permutations is symmetric.

The source notes that related distributions are not symmetric in small cases, but speculates that the polynomial $P_n(x,y)=\sum_{sigma\incmathfrak{S}_n}x^{\operatorname{des}_2(sigma)}y^{\operatorname{ear}(sigma)}$ is invariant under $x\leftrightarrow y$.
350200 Let $G_{n,p}$ be the binomial random graph with constant $p\in(0,1-1/e^2]$, let $Y_n=\chi(G_{n,p})$, and let $\sigma_n^2=\operatorname{Var}(Y_n)$. Define

$$
\widetilde w_n=\frac{n^{1/4}}{\log^{7/4}n}.
$$

**Best-case variance conjecture.** One has

$$
0<\liminf\frac{\sigma_n}{\widetilde w_n}<\infty.
$$

This is the proposed lower-order scale for the variance in the best case. The authors explicitly describe this conjecture as less certain than the worst-case statement and give no resolution.
350220 Let $w$ be an infinite word, and let $\operatorname{PPL}_w(n)$ denote the palindromic length of the prefix of $w$ of length $n$. **Frid's conjecture.** If $w$ is $k$-power-free for some positive integer $k$, then

$$
\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\operatorname{PPL}_w(n)}{\ln n}>0.
$$

This predicts logarithmic growth, along an infinite subsequence of prefix lengths, for the palindromic length of prefixes of every power-free infinite word.
350453 Let $P=(X,\prec)$ be a finite poset, let $\operatorname{\mathcal{E}}(P)$ be its set of linear extensions, and write $e(P)=|\operatorname{\mathcal{E}}(P)|$. **The one-third–two-thirds conjecture.** If $P$ is not totally ordered, then there are distinct elements $x,y\in X$ such that

$$
\frac13\leq \frac{|\\{L\in\operatorname{\mathcal{E}}(P):L(x)<L(y)\\}|}{e(P)}\leq\frac23.
$$

This conjecture is open in full generality, although it is known for several special classes, including posets of width two, where the bounds are tight.
350521 Let $(W,S)$ be a simply-laced triangle-free Coxeter system, and let ${\boldsymbol{\alpha}}$ be a reduced expression with link factorization

$$
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_1\mid {\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_2\mid\cdots\mid {\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_k.
$$

Write $B({\boldsymbol{\alpha}})$ for its braid graph, let $\dim_I$ denote isometric dimension, and let $\operatorname{rank}({\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_i)$ denote the rank of each link factor.

**Reduced-expression isometric-dimension conjecture.**

$$
\dim_I(B({\boldsymbol{\alpha}}))=\sum_{i=1}^k\operatorname{rank}({\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_i).
$$

This conjecture is presented as a consequence of the link dimension conjecture together with the product decomposition of braid graphs; it remains conditional and unproved in the supplied text.
350542 Let $Y_k(u)$ be the set of expanders with $k$ edges, $u-1$ white vertices and one black vertex. Let $X_k(i,j)$ be the set of expanders with $k$ edges, $i+j-2$ white vertices and two black vertices with weights $i-1$ and $j-1$. **Goulden–Rattan's square-coefficient conjecture.** For natural numbers $i\neq j$ and any natural number $k$,

$$
(2j-1)\left\|X_k(j,j)\right\|\geq (j-1)^2\left\|Y_k(2j)\right\|
$$

and

$$
(i+j-1)\left\|X_k(i,j)\right\|\geq 2(i-1)(j-1)\left\|Y_k(i+j)\right\|.
$$

These inequalities reformulate the Goulden–Rattan conjecture for square coefficients in terms of expanders. The supplied text does not state whether these inequalities have been proved or refuted, so their status remains open.
350549 Let $T$ be a tree. Let $CPA(T)$ and $CPD(T)$ denote the characteristic polynomials of its adjacency and distance matrices, respectively. Let $p_A(T)$ be the position of the peak of the absolute values of the non-zero coefficients of $CPA(T)$, and let $p_D(T)$ be the index of the peak of the normalized coefficients of $CPD(T)$. **Collins's conjecture.** The absolute values of the non-zero coefficients of $CPA(T)$ form a unimodal sequence, and its peak is at the same place as the peak of the normalized coefficients of $CPD(T)$.

The conjecture is presented as an open conjecture about the relationship between the two coefficient sequences for trees. The surrounding discussion gives supporting examples for paths and stars but does not establish the claim or provide a resolution.

  "345095": {

    "title": "The near-bipartite stability conjecture for low fractional triangle-packing density",
